### 0004 · CHATGPT · 2026-07-17T18:06:02Z

Yes, but **Google Workspace itself is not a full BPO dialer platform**.

Google offers **Google Voice** as an add-on to Google Workspace. It provides:

- Business phone numbers
- Incoming/outgoing calls
- IVR / Auto Attendant
- Ring groups
- Call forwarding
- Voicemail transcription
- Call transfer
- Call recording (higher plans)
- Basic reporting and administration citeturn0search0turn0search2turn0search1

**Good for:**
- Small business
- Customer support team (5–50 agents)
- Basic inbound call center
- Internal company telephony

**Not ideal for a BPO that needs:**
- Predictive Dialer
- Power Dialer
- Progressive Dialer
- Auto Campaign Management
- Agent Script Popups
- Lead Management
- Disposition Tracking
- Quality Monitoring
- Workforce Management
- Omnichannel Contact Center
- 100+ outbound telecallers

For the BPO company you were discussing earlier (voice, non-voice, SME to corporate), most companies use:

| Area | Common Products |
|--------|--------|
| Contact Center | urlGenesys Cloudhttps://www.genesys.com |
| Contact Center | urlFive9https://www.five9.com |
| Contact Center | urlNICE CXonehttps://www.nice.com |
| Contact Center | urlAmazon Connecthttps://aws.amazon.com/connect/ |
| SMB BPO | urlAircallhttps://aircall.io |
| SMB BPO | urlJustCallhttps://justcall.io |
| SMB BPO | urlDialpadhttps://www.dialpad.com |
| Open Source | urlVICIdialhttps://www.vicidial.org |
| Open Source | urlAsteriskhttps://www.asterisk.org |

### Recommended for your BPO startup

**0–20 agents**
- Google Workspace
- Google Voice (or JustCall/Aircall)
- CRM (HubSpot/Zoho)
- WhatsApp Business API

**20–100 agents**
- Google Workspace
- Amazon Connect or VICIdial
- Zoho CRM / Salesforce
- QA & Reporting

**100+ agents**
- Microsoft 365 or Google Workspace
- Genesys / Five9 / NICE
- Salesforce
- Workforce Management
- Speech Analytics

So the answer is:

✅ **Google Workspace + Google Voice can work as a small call center.**

❌ **Google Workspace alone is not a complete BPO dialer solution like VICIdial, Five9, Genesys, or Amazon Connect.**

If your agents are **sitting in India**, there are two separate questions:

1. **Can the software be used from India?**
2. **Can it legally provide Indian phone numbers and telecom services in India?**

Many international systems can be used from India via browser/headset, but Indian telecom regulations often require an Indian provider or approved connectivity.

# Google Voice / BPO Dialer in India — Step-by-Step

## 1. First understand the difference

There are three separate requirements:

1. **Agents are physically working from India**
2. **Agents call customers located in India**
3. **Agents call international customers from India**

A product may support one requirement but not the others.

For example, an international dialer may allow an agent in India to log in through a browser and call UK or US customers. That does **not automatically mean** it can legally provide Indian phone numbers or make domestic India-to-India calls.

---

# 2. Can Google Voice be purchased in India?

## Official position

Google Voice for business is officially offered only in selected countries, including:

- United Kingdom
- United States
- Canada
- Belgium
- Denmark
- France
- Germany
- Ireland
- Italy
- Netherlands
- Portugal
- Spain
- Sweden
- Switzerland

**India is not currently listed as a supported Google Voice country.** citeturn186050search3

Therefore, an Indian company generally cannot:

- Purchase Google Voice as an India-based service
- Obtain an Indian Google Voice telephone number
- Configure India as the official Google Voice service location
- Use Google Voice as its compliant India domestic call-centre platform

---

# 3. What happens if the company is registered in the UK or US?

Suppose you have:

- A UK company
- Google Workspace registered in the UK
- UK Google Voice numbers
- Employees working remotely from India

Technically, an employee may sometimes be able to sign in from India and use the Google Voice browser or mobile application.

However, that does **not make Google Voice an officially supported Indian contact-centre service**.

You must consider:

- Google’s supported-location rules
- Emergency calling limitations
- Number residency requirements
- Indian telecom regulations
- International and domestic call routing
- Call-recording laws
- Customer-consent requirements
- Client contractual requirements

So it should not be treated as a reliable long-term workaround for an Indian BPO.

## Practical interpretation

| Scenario | Google Voice suitability |
|---|---:|
| UK employee temporarily travelling in India | Possibly usable, subject to service restrictions |
| One or two India-based employees making occasional UK calls | Technically possible in some setups, but verify with Google |
| Permanent India-based BPO agents | Not recommended |
| Indian customer-support number | Not available |
| India-to-India business calling | Not suitable |
| Predictive outbound campaign | Not supported |
| 20–100 agent BPO | Not suitable |
| UK office phone system | Suitable |
| Small UK business with basic calling | Suitable |

---

# 4. Can an India-based agent call international customers?

Yes. This is possible with several international and Indian contact-centre providers.

An agent sitting in India can use:

- A web browser
- USB headset
- Desktop application
- Mobile application
- WebRTC softphone

They can call customers in:

- UK
- US
- Canada
- Australia
- Europe
- Middle East
- Other supported countries

However, the phone number shown to the customer depends on the provider.

For example:

- UK customer may see a UK number
- US customer may see a US number
- UAE customer may see a UAE number
- Indian customer should generally see an authorised Indian business number

The provider may require:

- Company registration documents
- Director identification
- Address proof
- Local-country business justification
- Client contract
- Regulatory-use declaration

JustCall, for example, offers numbers across numerous international markets, but documentation requirements differ by country. citeturn186050search15turn186050search27

---

# 5. Google Voice features

Google Voice is mainly a **business phone system**, not a complete BPO dialer.

## Available features

Depending on the plan, it can include:

- Business phone number
- Incoming calls
- Outgoing calls
- Voicemail
- Voicemail transcription
- Call forwarding
- Call transfer
- Call recording
- Ring groups
- Auto attendants
- Desk-phone support
- Usage and activity reporting
- Google Workspace integration

Google advertises business plans starting at approximately **US$10 per user per month**, with higher tiers around US$20 and US$30 per user per month. citeturn186050search20turn186050search22

## Missing BPO features

Google Voice does not provide a complete set of features normally required by a serious BPO:

- Predictive dialler
- Progressive dialler
- Power dialler
- Multiple outbound campaigns
- Lead recycling
- Campaign-specific scripts
- Agent disposition codes
- Advanced supervisor wallboard
- Workforce management
- Quality scorecards
- Complex skills-based routing
- Multi-client tenant isolation
- Full omnichannel operations
- Extensive outbound compliance controls
- Advanced speech analytics
- Dedicated BPO billing by client

That is why Google Voice may suit a company office but not a telecalling operation.

---

# 6. India and international provider comparison

## Core comparison

| Product | India-based agents | Indian number | International numbers | India-to-India | International outbound | Predictive dialler | Best use |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Google Voice | Limited/unsupported deployment | No | Yes, supported countries | No practical support | Yes, from supported setup | No | Small overseas office |
| Exotel | Yes | Yes | Selected markets/integrations | Yes | Yes | Available by product/configuration | Indian SME and enterprise |
| Knowlarity | Yes | Yes | Selected regions | Yes | Yes | Yes on suitable plan | Indian inbound/outbound teams |
| Airtel IQ | Yes | Yes | Enterprise arrangements | Yes | Yes | Available through contact-centre offering | Indian enterprise |
| JustCall | Yes | Subject to availability | Yes | Needs verification for Indian domestic routing | Yes | Yes | International sales/support |
| Amazon Connect | Yes | Requires supported telephony setup/partner | Yes | Requires India-compliant connectivity | Yes | Custom/outbound campaigns | Technical, scalable BPO |
| VICIdial | Yes | Through authorised telecom/SIP provider | Yes | Through authorised provider | Yes | Yes | Low-cost custom BPO |
| Zoho Voice | Restricted for India domestic use | No Indian local number | Yes | No, from India | India-based user can call countries other than India | Basic dialling/contact centre | International-only Zoho teams |

Zoho explicitly states that users in India cannot use Zoho Voice to make calls within India, but may purchase another-country number and make calls to countries other than India. citeturn663124search30

---

# 7. Pricing comparison

Prices below should be treated as **planning figures**, not final quotations. Telecom costs change based on:

- Number type
- Country
- Call destination
- Inbound versus outbound
- Mobile versus landline
- Concurrent calls
- Named agents
- Recording storage
- WhatsApp usage
- SMS volume
- Minimum commitment
- GST
- Support level

## Google Voice

| Plan | Approximate licence |
|---|---:|
| Starter | US$10/user/month |
| Standard | US$20/user/month |
| Premier | US$30/user/month |

Additional international call charges may apply based on the originating and destination countries. Google publishes separate international calling rates. citeturn186050search0turn186050search2

|---|---:|

But this is **not a valid India contact-centre recommendation**, because India is not a supported Google Voice service country.

## Exotel

Exotel offers:

- Indian virtual numbers
- IVR
- Call recordings
- Reports
- Auto dialling
- Voice APIs
- Call transfer
- SMS campaigns
- WhatsApp APIs
- Call campaigns

Its enterprise contact-centre pricing is generally quotation-based. citeturn186050search4turn186050search10

A public third-party 2026 guide reports a small-business starting package of approximately ₹9,999 for five months for three agents, but this should not be used as an enterprise BPO quotation. citeturn186050search24

### Planning estimate

For budgeting, reserve approximately:

| Team size | Broad monthly planning range |
|---|---:|
| 3–5 agents | ₹5,000–₹20,000 plus usage |
| 10 agents | ₹20,000–₹60,000 plus usage |
| 20 agents | ₹40,000–₹1,20,000 plus usage |
| 50 agents | Custom enterprise quotation |

The actual quotation can differ substantially.

---

## JustCall

JustCall advertises plans starting around US$19–US$29 per user per month depending on the product and current plan structure. Higher outbound, AI and automation capabilities cost more. citeturn186050search5turn186050search25

It offers:

- International phone numbers
- Power dialling
- Multi-line dialling on selected plans
- SMS
- CRM integrations
- Analytics
- AI features
- Workflow automation
- Call recording
- Call monitoring
- Coaching features

JustCall publishes an indicative rate of approximately **US$0.05 per minute for calls to India**, although the exact cost depends on origin, destination, number type and plan. citeturn186050search13turn186050search14

### Example: 20 agents

Assuming US$29 per agent:

- Licence: 20 × US$29 = **US$580/month**
- Approximate INR equivalent: roughly **₹50,000/month**, depending on exchange rate
- Call charges, numbers, SMS, AI and taxes are additional

On higher plans, the total may easily become ₹1 lakh or more per month.

---

## Amazon Connect

Amazon Connect uses a **pay-as-you-go model** instead of a standard per-agent licence.

AWS states that there is:

- No upfront commitment
- No fixed capacity requirement
- No conventional seat-based licence for the base usage model
- Usage-based billing citeturn186050search6

You pay separately for elements such as:

- Contact-centre service usage
- Inbound call minutes
- Outbound call minutes
- Telephone numbers
- Recording storage
- Transcription
- AI assistance
- Tasks
- Chat
- Messaging
- Email
- Analytics
- AWS data services

### Important

Amazon Connect is not necessarily automatically cheap.

It may be economical when:

- Agents have low or irregular call volume
- The operation is seasonal
- You need rapid scaling
- You already have AWS expertise
- You automate routing and self-service

It may become expensive when:

- Calls are long
- Recording and transcription are enabled for everything
- Multiple AI services are added
- Architecture is poorly controlled
- International telephony is expensive
- You require extensive consulting support

---

## VICIdial

VICIdial itself is open-source software.

You still pay for:

- Cloud or physical servers
- Database server
- Backup
- Security
- SIP or telecom connectivity
- Phone numbers
- Call usage
- Recording storage
- System administrator
- Developer/customisation
- Monitoring
- High availability
- Disaster recovery

### Planning costs

| Component | Possible monthly planning range |
|---|---:|
| Basic server | ₹5,000–₹15,000 |
| Production servers | ₹20,000–₹75,000+ |
| Backup/storage | ₹5,000–₹30,000+ |
| Technical support | ₹20,000–₹1,00,000+ |
| Telecom/SIP usage | Based on call volume |
| Setup/customisation | One-time ₹50,000 to several lakhs |

VICIdial can be cheaper at scale, but only when you have good technical and telecom support.

---

# 8. India telecom and BPO compliance

India permits remote agents and work-from-anywhere arrangements for OSP operations under revised Department of Telecommunications guidelines. Remote agents may connect using broadband or wireless technology. citeturn663124search0

However, this does not mean that any foreign VoIP product can be used without review.

You should verify:

- Whether the telecom connection is supplied by an authorised provider
- Whether Indian PSTN and foreign network traffic are being improperly interconnected
- Whether call records must be retained
- Whether the caller ID is authorised
- Whether recording consent is required
- Whether client data may leave India
- Whether the client requires local data storage
- Whether PCI DSS applies
- Whether health, banking or financial regulations apply
- Whether DND/TCCCP requirements apply to promotional calls
- Whether the operation is transactional, service-related or promotional

For a real launch, get written confirmation from:

1. The chosen telecom/contact-centre provider  
2. Your Indian legal or telecom-compliance adviser  
3. The client whose calling process you will operate  

---

# 9. Correct setup for different business models

## Scenario A: Indian customers, Indian BPO agents

Example:

- Agents in Tamil Nadu
- Calling Indian customers
- Indian customer-support number
- Tamil/Hindi/English support

### Recommended stack

- **Google Workspace** for email, Drive, Meet and documents
- **Exotel, Knowlarity or Airtel IQ** for calling
- **Zoho CRM or Zoho Desk** for customer records
- **WhatsApp Business Platform**
- **Looker Studio or Power BI** for reporting

### Why?

Because you need:

- Indian telephone numbers
- Indian caller ID
- Domestic routing
- Local compliance
- Call recording
- IVR
- Agent controls
- Campaign reports

Google Voice should not be used as the primary dialler.

---

## Scenario B: India agents serving UK customers

Example:

- Agents work from India
- Client is a UK company
- Customers are in the UK
- UK phone number is required

### Possible options

- JustCall
- Aircall
- Dialpad
- Amazon Connect
- Genesys Cloud
- Five9
- UK telecom provider integrated with a contact centre
- India-compliant enterprise voice partner

### Required checks

- Can the provider issue a UK number?
- Can permanent India-based agents use it?
- Is the number portable?
- Are inbound and outbound calls supported?
- Is emergency calling disabled or restricted?
- Is UK GDPR addressed?
- Where are recordings stored?
- Does the UK client permit offshore processing?
- Is there a data-processing agreement?
- Is PCI-compliant pause-and-resume recording required?

---

## Scenario C: India agents serving US customers

You will normally require:

- US local or toll-free numbers
- US calling capability
- Time-zone scheduling
- TCPA compliance for campaigns
- Do-not-call controls
- Consent management
- Call-recording rules by state
- CRM integration
- Approved outbound caller identity

A sales campaign cannot simply upload purchased leads and run a predictive dialler. Consent and campaign classification matter.

---

## Scenario D: Mixed Indian and international customers

This is the likely model for your planned BPO.

Do not force every client onto one telephone provider.

Use a **multi-provider architecture**:

| Requirement | Provider category |
|---|---|
| India domestic calling | Exotel, Knowlarity, Airtel IQ or similar |
| UK client campaigns | International CCaaS or UK carrier |
| US client campaigns | International CCaaS or US carrier |
| Low-cost custom outbound | VICIdial plus authorised connectivity |
| Enterprise omnichannel | Amazon Connect, Genesys, NICE or Five9 |
| Email and documents | Google Workspace |
| CRM | Zoho, HubSpot, Freshsales or Salesforce |
| Helpdesk | Zoho Desk, Freshdesk, Zendesk |
| WhatsApp | Approved BSP/API provider |

---

# 10. Step-by-step implementation plan

## Step 1: Define the first process

Do not buy software before defining the first client process.

Document:

- Inbound or outbound
- India or international
- Sales, support, collection, recruitment or survey
- Expected call volume
- Average call duration
- Number of agents
- Working hours
- Required languages
- Recording requirements
- CRM requirement
- WhatsApp and email requirement
- Client reporting requirement

Example:

> Ten India-based agents making outbound appointment-setting calls to UK small businesses, Monday to Friday, using a UK number, recording every call and updating Zoho CRM.

That definition determines the platform.

---

## Step 2: Separate collaboration from telephony

### Collaboration layer

Use Google Workspace for:

- Employee email
- Shared drives
- Client folders
- Google Meet
- Google Chat
- Policies
- Training documents
- Daily trackers
- Quality forms

### Telephony layer

Use a dedicated provider for:

- Numbers
- Calls
- IVR
- Dialling
- Recording
- Campaigns
- Supervisor monitoring
- Call reports

Google Workspace and the dialler are two separate systems.

---

## Step 3: Decide India, international or mixed telephony

### India-only

Shortlist:

- Exotel
- Knowlarity
- Airtel IQ
- Other authorised Indian providers

### International-only

Shortlist:

- JustCall
- Amazon Connect
- Genesys
- Five9
- Aircall
- Dialpad

### Mixed

Use:

- Indian provider for domestic calls
- International provider for overseas campaigns

This is safer than trying to route everything through one foreign account.

---

## Step 4: Ask each vendor the same questions

Send a structured requirement sheet.

Ask:

1. Can India-based agents use the service permanently?
2. Can you issue Indian numbers?
3. Can you issue UK and US numbers?
4. Can the same agent switch between client campaigns?
5. Is predictive dialling included?
6. Is power dialling included?
7. Is call recording included?
8. How long are recordings retained?
9. Is supervisor live monitoring included?
10. Are whisper and barge-in included?
11. Are disposition codes supported?
12. Is lead management included?
13. Is CRM integration included?
14. Is WhatsApp included?
15. Is there an API?
16. Is there a minimum commitment?
17. Are charges based on named or concurrent agents?
18. What are inbound and outbound per-minute rates?
19. What is the number rental?
20. What is the implementation charge?
21. What support SLA is provided?
22. Where is data stored?
23. What compliance documentation is provided?
24. Is the solution suitable for Indian OSP/BPO operations?
25. Can they confirm this in writing?

---

## Step 5: Calculate the real monthly cost

Do not compare only the agent licence.

Use:

> **Total monthly cost = licences + telephone numbers + call usage + recording + storage + CRM + implementation amortisation + support + taxes**

### Example: 20-agent operation

Assume:

- 20 agents
- 120 minutes per agent per day
- 22 working days
- 52,800 monthly call minutes

Then compare:

#### Provider A

- Licence: ₹60,000
- Calling: ₹31,680
- Number rental: ₹5,000
- Recording/storage: ₹5,000
- GST and extras: additional

Approximate pre-tax total: **₹1,01,680**

#### Provider B

- Licence: ₹40,000
- Calling: ₹52,800
- Number rental: ₹8,000
- Recording: ₹10,000

Approximate pre-tax total: **₹1,10,800**

Provider A has a higher licence but a lower total cost.

---

## Step 6: Run a five-agent pilot

Do not immediately purchase 50 licences.

Start with:

- One client process
- Five agents
- One supervisor
- One number
- One CRM workflow
- One call script
- One QA scorecard
- Two to four weeks of controlled testing

Measure:

- Connection rate
- Answer rate
- Average handling time
- First-call resolution
- Conversion rate
- Cost per connected call
- Cost per sale
- Recording quality
- Internet stability
- Agent productivity
- Customer complaints
- Reporting accuracy

---

## Step 7: Test remote-agent infrastructure

Each agent should have:

- Dedicated laptop or desktop
- USB noise-cancelling headset
- Stable broadband
- Backup mobile internet
- Quiet workspace
- Updated browser
- Endpoint protection
- Device-management policy
- No personal recording
- Controlled access to client data

Recommended operational target:

- Wired connection where possible
- At least 10 Mbps stable connection per agent
- Low latency and jitter
- UPS or power backup
- Secondary connectivity

The provider’s minimum requirement may be lower, but a BPO should design above the minimum.

---

## Step 8: Configure the contact-centre workflow

For inbound support:

1. Customer calls the business number
2. IVR identifies language or department
3. Call enters the correct queue
4. Skills-based routing selects an agent
5. CRM record opens
6. Agent answers
7. Call is recorded
8. Agent enters notes and disposition
9. Ticket or follow-up task is created
10. Supervisor receives reports

For outbound sales:

1. Approved leads are uploaded
2. Leads are checked against applicable consent/DND requirements
3. Campaign is created
4. Agents log in
5. Dialler places calls
6. CRM screen opens
7. Agent follows the script
8. Outcome is recorded
9. Follow-up SMS/email is triggered
10. Results are reported to the client

---

## Step 9: Create client separation

Because you plan to serve multiple companies, each client should have:

- Separate campaign
- Separate number
- Separate call script
- Separate CRM pipeline
- Separate recordings
- Separate agent permissions
- Separate quality scorecard
- Separate reports
- Separate billing
- Separate data-retention policy

Do not mix leads and recordings across clients.

---

## Step 10: Scale only after the pilot

### Phase 1: 1–10 agents

Recommended:

- Google Workspace
- Knowlarity or Exotel
- Zoho CRM/Desk
- WhatsApp Business
- Simple QA forms

### Phase 2: 10–50 agents

Add:

- Advanced dialling
- Supervisor monitoring
- Workforce scheduling
- Automated QA
- Client dashboards
- Dedicated IT support
- Backup internet
- Role-based access

### Phase 3: 50–200 agents

Consider:

- Amazon Connect
- Genesys
- NICE
- Five9
- Enterprise Indian CCaaS provider
- Separate staging and production environments
- High availability
- Security audits
- Formal SLA management
- Dedicated compliance officer

---

# Recommended decision for your BPO

## For Indian domestic projects

Start with:

- **Google Workspace**
- **Knowlarity or Exotel**
- **Zoho CRM/Zoho Desk**
- **WhatsApp Business Platform**

Knowlarity currently provides the clearest public per-agent pricing among the options reviewed, while Exotel may provide a stronger API and broader custom communication platform depending on your requirements. citeturn663124search9turn186050search10

## For international projects

Shortlist:

- **JustCall** for a faster small-team launch
- **Amazon Connect** for custom scalable operations
- **Genesys/Five9/NICE** for enterprise clients

## Do not use Google Voice as the main India BPO dialler

Use Google Workspace for the company’s collaboration system, but choose a proper telephony/contact-centre provider for calling.

The safest initial design is:

> **Google Workspace + Indian contact-centre provider + Zoho CRM/Desk + WhatsApp Business + client-specific reporting.**

# Indian BPO Dialler & Contact-Centre Products — Detailed Comparison

**Research date: 17 July 2026**

The Indian market has two different categories:

1. **Business cloud-telephony systems** — virtual number, IVR, routing, recordings and small call teams.
2. **Full contact-centre platforms** — inbound/outbound campaigns, diallers, supervisors, quality monitoring, omnichannel support, APIs and enterprise reporting.

For your planned BPO, a cheap virtual-number product alone is insufficient. You need a platform that can separate multiple client campaigns, recordings, numbers, agents and reports.

---

## 1. Executive comparison

| Provider | Best suited for | Published price | India calling | International capability | Overall BPO suitability |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **Tata Tele Smartflo** | Indian outbound/inbound BPO needing predictable domestic calling costs | From ₹950–₹1,200 per licence/concurrency, plus setup depending on product | Excellent | Verify destination and international routing | **Very strong** |
| **Knowlarity** | 5–100 agent Indian sales/support operation | ₹1,999–₹3,499 per agent/month | Excellent | Available subject to plan and country | **Strong** |
| **Exotel** | API-driven BPO, customer support, fintech, e-commerce and enterprise communication | Small business package ₹19,999; enterprise custom | Excellent | Strong through Exotel’s international operations, subject to number availability | **Very strong** |
| **Ozonetel** | Medium/large omnichannel BPO, AI and enterprise CX | Custom quotation | Excellent | Yes; enterprise deployment | **Excellent** |
| **Ameyo / Exotel** | Enterprise, banks, healthcare, collections, omnichannel and complex routing | Custom quotation | Excellent | Yes | **Excellent** |
| **Airtel IQ** | Indian enterprise requiring carrier-backed connectivity and APIs | Custom quotation | Excellent | Enterprise arrangements | **Very strong** |
| **MyOperator** | SMEs, distributed calling teams, franchise/support operations | Some products custom; enterprise virtual call centre advertised from ₹2 lakh/month | Excellent | Verify per-country availability | **Moderate to strong** |
| **TeleCMI** | Cost-sensitive small/mid-sized teams and international operations | Entry business plans from ₹587/month; virtual CC from US$14/user/month | Yes | Strong international-number positioning | **Moderate to strong** |

Pricing is not directly comparable because some companies charge **per named user**, some **per concurrent call**, some bundle calling minutes, and others charge separately for licences, numbers and minutes.

---

# 2. Price comparison

## Published pricing

| Provider/product | Published price | What the price appears to include | Important limitation |
|---|---:|---|---|
| **Knowlarity Inbound Contact Centre** | **₹1,999/month** | Inbound contact-centre package | Confirm whether price is per agent, taxes, number rental and usage |
| **Knowlarity Inbound + Outbound** | **₹2,999/month** | Inbound and outbound contact centre | Confirm minute allowance and dialler type |
| **Knowlarity with Tentacle Lead Management** | **₹3,499/month** | Inbound, outbound and lead-management functions | CRM, integrations and implementation may cost extra |
| **Exotel Believer business-phone package** | **₹19,999 for 11 months** | ₹10,499 rental, 9,500 communication credits, two virtual numbers, six agents and multilevel IVR | This is a business-phone package, not necessarily the full enterprise BPO contact-centre product |
| **Tata Smartflo ITS** | **₹1,000/licence/month** | Softphone calling, one standard DID per agent, six-month recordings and advanced reports | ₹10,000 one-time cost; fair-usage and tenure conditions apply |
| **Tata Smartflo OBD Unlimited** | **₹1,200/concurrency/month** | Outbound dialling, Indian calling and advanced reporting | ₹10,000 one-time cost; no call recording shown for this product |
| **Tata Smartflo Voice Streaming Unlimited** | **₹1,100/concurrency/month** | Inbound and outbound PSTN calling, DID per concurrency and six-month recording | ₹20,000 one-time cost |
| **Tata Smartflo API Click-to-Call** | **₹25,000 / ₹1 lakh / ₹5 lakh monthly platform tiers** | Usage-based API calling with standard DID and recording | ₹20,000 one-time cost; calling usage is additional |
| **TeleCMI business-phone entry level** | **From ₹587/month**, minimum three users | Basic IVR, routing, voicemail, transfers and recording according to its pricing guide | Not equivalent to a full predictive-dialler contact centre |
| **TeleCMI virtual call centre** | **From US$14/user/month** | Cloud call-centre functions | Usage, numbers, advanced integrations and taxes may be additional |
| **MyOperator enterprise virtual call centre** | **Advertised from ₹2,00,000/month** | Enterprise virtual contact-centre deployment | Exact agent count, usage and included modules require quotation |

Knowlarity currently publishes the most understandable agent-level package pricing. Tata Smartflo provides some of the clearest domestic concurrency/licence pricing. Exotel publishes a small business-phone package but quotes its full enterprise contact-centre system separately. citeturn514438search12turn514438search23turn756877search5turn756877search30turn756877search16

---

## Quotation-only enterprise products

| Provider | Pricing status | What must be requested in the quotation |
|---|---|---|
| **Exotel Enterprise Contact Centre** | Custom quotation | Agent licence, concurrency, number rental, inbound/outbound rates, recording, storage, WhatsApp, API and implementation |
| **Ozonetel CloudAgent / CX platform** | Custom quotation | Voice seats, dialler, omnichannel, AI, WhatsApp, QA, CRM integration, data retention and support SLA |
| **Ameyo** | Custom quotation | Voice, outbound dialler, omnichannel, ticketing, recording, QA, deployment model and implementation |
| **Airtel IQ** | Custom quotation | Number/DID, concurrency, API use, domestic minutes, international calls, recording and support |
| **MyOperator full call-centre deployment** | Primarily quotation-based | Agents, numbers, campaigns, outbound dialling, integrations and WhatsApp |
| **Tata Smartflo advanced enterprise configuration** | Published components plus quotation | Correct licence/concurrency model, usage rates, integrations and enterprise SLA |

A quotation-only product is not automatically more expensive. For 50–200 agents, a negotiated concurrency or pooled-usage agreement can be cheaper than buying a licence for every employee.

- Call routing and recording

The **Believer** package is ₹19,999 for eleven months, containing ₹10,499 rental, 9,500 credits, two Exophones, six agents and multilevel IVR. This package is useful for small operations, but your BPO should separately obtain an enterprise-contact-centre quotation. citeturn514438search23turn514438search18turn514438search28

Use Exotel where:

- Clients need API integrations
- Calls trigger CRM or workflow actions

- You expect to build custom client solutions
- You need a provider that can grow beyond a simple dialler

- Does the quote include a predictive dialler?
- Is billing per agent or concurrency?
- Are domestic minutes bundled?

## C. Tata Tele Business Services — Smartflo

The advertised unlimited model is subject to a fair-usage policy of **5,000 minutes per user per month, pooled**. Some licences may require a minimum three-month tenure. citeturn756877search1turn756877search5

### Best use

- High-volume India calls
- Domestic customer support
- Collections and reminders
- Outbound lead campaigns
- Businesses that value carrier accountability
- BPOs wanting bundled domestic calling rather than unpredictable minute charges

### Major warning

“Per concurrency” and “per agent” are different:

- **20 named agents with 10 concurrent calls** may require only 10 concurrency licences for certain products.
- But each agent may still require a software login or separate product licence.

Ask Tata to produce a full bill for your exact staffing and peak-call model.

# 5. Estimated monthly cost for 10, 20 and 50 agents

These calculations cover **platform licence only where public pricing exists**. Calls, numbers, GST, onboarding, CRM and add-ons may be extra.

## Licence/platform illustration

| Provider/plan | 10 agents | 20 agents | 50 agents | Notes |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Knowlarity inbound ₹1,999 | ₹19,990 | ₹39,980 | ₹99,950 | Usage and extras to verify |
| Knowlarity inbound/outbound ₹2,999 | ₹29,990 | ₹59,980 | ₹1,49,950 | Suitable public baseline |
| Knowlarity + lead management ₹3,499 | ₹34,990 | ₹69,980 | ₹1,74,950 | Lead management included |
| Tata Smartflo ITS ₹1,000 | ₹10,000 | ₹20,000 | ₹50,000 | Add ₹10,000 one-time setup |
| Tata OBD at one concurrency per active agent | ₹12,000 | ₹24,000 | ₹60,000 | Add ₹10,000 one-time setup |
| Tata Voice Streaming at one concurrency per active agent | ₹11,000 | ₹22,000 | ₹55,000 | Add ₹20,000 one-time setup |
| TeleCMI at US$14/user | US$140 | US$280 | US$700 | Number, usage and taxes may be additional |
| Exotel Believer small package | ₹19,999 total package | Not an appropriate 20-agent basis | Not appropriate | Designed for six agents; request enterprise quote |

A concurrency model can cost less than the table when not all agents talk simultaneously. For example, 20 employees working with only 12 simultaneous calls may require 12 concurrency units rather than 20, depending on the vendor’s licensing rules.

# 6. International calling from India

## Can India-based agents use these products for international calls?

| Provider | India agent calling UK/US customers | Foreign number availability | Recommendation |

| Exotel | Yes, subject to solution and regulatory design | Available in supported markets | Strong for mixed India/international operations |
| Knowlarity | Possible; obtain country-specific confirmation | Selected markets/products | Suitable when India remains the main operation |

| Ameyo | Yes, using appropriate telecom layer | Yes through enterprise architecture | Strong for international enterprise BPO |
| Airtel IQ | Possible through enterprise agreement | Custom | Ask Airtel for destination/number coverage |
| MyOperator | Verify destination and foreign-number coverage | Selected/custom | Better for India-first operation |
| TeleCMI | Yes; international calling is a major proposition | Yes, advertised across countries | Strong small/mid-market international candidate |

Do not assume that purchasing a UK or US virtual number automatically authorises every type of outbound sales campaign. Number regulations, customer consent, call-recording laws and telemarketing rules still apply.

# 7. Recommended shortlist for your BPO

## Option 1 — Cheapest structured India launch

### **Tata Smartflo + Zoho CRM/Desk + Google Workspace**

Best for:

- 10–30 India-based agents
- Domestic inbound and outbound calls
- Predictable calling costs
- Basic support, sales, recruitment and appointment campaigns

- Carrier-backed service
- Recording on selected products
- Lower apparent licence cost

Risk:

- You must confirm predictive dialler, campaign depth, QA and multi-client controls.

---

## Option 2 — Fastest simple launch

### **Knowlarity + Zoho CRM + Google Workspace**

Best for:

- 5–20 agents
- Indian sales/support
- Limited internal IT capability
- Clear initial budgeting

Why:

- Straightforward public pricing
- India virtual numbers
- Inbound/outbound plans
- Lead-management option

Risk:

- Enterprise omnichannel and advanced BPO management may require upgrades or integrations.

---

## Option 3 — Best technical growth platform

### **Exotel + Zoho/Salesforce/Freshdesk + Google Workspace**

Best for:

- 10–100 agents
- API-integrated operations
- India and international clients
- Voice, SMS and WhatsApp
- Custom client workflows

Why:

- Strong communications APIs
- Enterprise contact-centre capability
- Broad product portfolio
- Suitable for creating client-specific solutions

Risk:

- Obtain a detailed quotation; avoid buying disconnected modules without total-cost modelling.

---

## Option 4 — Best enterprise BPO platform

### **Ozonetel or Ameyo**

Best for:

- 50–500+ agents
- Multiple clients
- Omnichannel service
- Banks, healthcare, insurance and major enterprise contracts
- AI automation
- Advanced supervisor and campaign operations

Why:

- Deeper contact-centre functionality
- Enterprise workflows
- Strong omnichannel positioning
- Better fit for complex operations

Risk:

- Higher implementation complexity and quotation-only pricing.

---

# 8. My recommendation for your specific BPO plan

Because you are planning:

- Voice and non-voice BPO
- Inbound and outbound
- Indian and international clients
- Social media and WhatsApp support
- E-commerce and IT support
- SME through corporate customers
- Remote/home-based workforce

Do **not** select only one provider immediately.

Use a two-stage evaluation:

| Stage | Providers to evaluate | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| **Startup pilot — 5 agents** | Knowlarity, Tata Smartflo and Exotel | Compare India domestic call quality, reports, recordings and total cost |
| **Enterprise readiness** | Ozonetel, Ameyo and Exotel Enterprise | Assess multi-client separation, omnichannel, QA, AI and scalability |
| **International pilot** | Exotel, Ozonetel and TeleCMI | UK/US numbers, international rates and India-agent eligibility |
| **Carrier/API requirement** | Airtel IQ and Tata Smartflo | Telecom reliability, API connectivity and enterprise support |

## Initial ranking

| Rank | Provider | Reason |
|---:|---|---|
| **1** | Exotel | Best balance of Indian telecom, APIs, omnichannel growth and enterprise readiness |
| **2** | Ozonetel | Strong full contact-centre and AI platform for medium/large BPO |
| **3** | Tata Smartflo | Attractive published India calling prices and carrier backing |
| **4** | Knowlarity | Simple and transparent for a small India launch |
| **5** | Ameyo | Strong enterprise system but likely more complex for the first pilot |
| **6** | Airtel IQ | Excellent carrier/API option; full dialler feature set needs confirmation |
| **7** | TeleCMI | Worth testing for international and price-sensitive teams |
| **8** | MyOperator | Good SME solution, but verify advanced BPO dialler and multi-client depth |

---

# 9. Information to send vendors for a comparable quotation

Send exactly the same requirement to all shortlisted providers:

> We are establishing a multi-client BPO in India. Initially, we require 10 agents and one supervisor, scaling to 50 agents within 12 months. The system must support Indian inbound and outbound calls, UK/US international campaigns, IVR, call recording, power and predictive dialling, CRM integration, WhatsApp, supervisor live monitoring, whisper/barge, campaign-level reporting, client-level data separation, APIs and remote agents.  
>  
> Please provide itemised pricing for licences, concurrency, Indian numbers, UK/US numbers, inbound/outbound minutes, recordings, storage, implementation, CRM integration, WhatsApp, AI modules, supervisor licences, support, taxes and minimum contract term. Please also confirm Indian telecom compliance, data location, SLA and recording-retention period.

## Do not accept a quotation without these columns

| Cost item | Required |
|---|---:|
| One-time implementation | Yes |
| Agent licence | Yes |
| Supervisor licence | Yes |
| Concurrent-call charge | Yes |
| Indian number/DID rental | Yes |
| Toll-free rental and incoming minute rate | Yes |
| Domestic outbound minute rate | Yes |
| UK/US number rental | Yes |
| International outbound rates | Yes |
| Call-recording storage | Yes |
| CRM integration | Yes |
| WhatsApp platform fee | Yes |
| Meta message charges | Yes |
| Dialler module | Yes |
| AI/speech analytics | Yes |
| Technical support/SLA | Yes |
| GST | Yes |
| Contract lock-in | Yes |
| Exit and number-porting cost | Yes |

The practical first shortlist is **Exotel, Tata Smartflo, Knowlarity and Ozonetel**. That gives you one API-led platform, one carrier-backed platform, one simple low-entry platform and one enterprise contact-centre platform for a proper pilot comparison.

### 0023 · CHATGPT · 2026-07-17T18:26:46Z

# 17/07/2026 18:58 — BPO Pricing & Productisation

Partner, your confusion is happening because **four different decisions are being mixed together**:

1. Which dialler/software to purchase  
2. How much the BPO operation costs  
3. How much to charge each client  
4. How to present the service as a simple product on the website  

These should not be solved simultaneously.

Your earlier discussion already identified many necessary costs—employee salary, dialler, software, rent, accounting, licences, company email, marketing, training, management salary, investment recovery and profit—but they have not yet been converted into one calculation model. fileciteturn0file0

---

# 1. First decide what company you are building

You are **not selling dialler software**.

You are selling an **outsourced business operation**.

The client does not primarily care whether you use Exotel, Knowlarity, Tata Smartflo or Ozonetel. The client cares about the outcome:

- Calls answered
- Leads generated
- Appointments booked
- Orders confirmed
- Customer issues resolved
- Candidates screened
- Payments followed up
- WhatsApp messages handled
- Reports submitted
- Work completed accurately

Therefore:

> **Software is your production tool. Service outcome is your product.**

Do not create the website service page around software names.

---

# 2. Separate the business into service families

Your planned company should initially have five clear product families.

| Product family | What you deliver | Suitable clients |
|---|---|---|
| **Customer Support Desk** | Inbound calls, email, WhatsApp and ticket support | E-commerce, clinics, education, SMEs |
| **Outbound Growth Desk** | Lead calling, qualification, appointments and follow-up | Real estate, finance, training, service businesses |
| **Back-Office Operations** | Data entry, order processing, document checking and reporting | SMEs, logistics, e-commerce, professional firms |
| **Recruitment Support Desk** | Candidate sourcing, screening, interview scheduling and follow-up | SMEs, consultancies, growing companies |
| **Managed Business Desk** | Combination of calls, WhatsApp, email, CRM and reports | Businesses without an internal support team |

Do not launch 25 individual services separately. These five families can contain the individual services.

---

# 3. Productise each service

A service becomes a product only when the buyer can understand:

- What is included
- How much work will be done
- What result will be reported
- What is not included
- How much it costs
- How long the commitment lasts

For example, “telecalling service” is too vague.

A proper product is:

## Lead Qualification Desk

| Element | Definition |
|---|---|
| Objective | Contact leads and identify genuinely interested prospects |
| Input from client | Lead list, business information, qualification rules |
| Work performed | Calling, follow-up, disposition and CRM update |
| Deliverable | Qualified-lead list and daily/weekly report |
| Performance metrics | Attempts, connections, qualified leads, follow-ups |
| Billing unit | Dedicated agent, productive hour or campaign |
| Minimum commitment | One month or agreed campaign period |
| Exclusions | Lead generation, advertising and sales closure unless added |

This can be priced and sold.

---

# 4. Do not create one universal pricing method

Different services require different pricing structures.

## Recommended pricing methods

| Service type | Best pricing method | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Dedicated customer-support agent | Monthly dedicated-agent price | Client is reserving capacity |
| Shared customer support | Monthly package with included volume | Several clients share the team |
| Outbound lead qualification | Monthly agent/campaign fee plus performance incentive | Call outcome varies |
| Appointment booking | Base fee plus qualified appointment fee | Protects both parties |
| Recruitment screening | Per candidate plus minimum campaign fee | Volume can fluctuate |
| Data entry | Per record, page, transaction or productive hour | Output is countable |
| WhatsApp/email support | Monthly conversation/ticket band | Work is transaction-based |
| Full managed desk | Monthly retainer | Multiple channels and tasks are combined |
| Short pilot | Fixed project price | Easy for a new client to test |

---

# 5. Build the cost before deciding the selling price

You cannot decide package prices by seeing competitors alone.

First calculate the **fully loaded cost of one productive employee**.

## Monthly cost per employee

| Cost category | Example |
|---|---:|
| Salary | ₹15,000 |
| Employer contributions/benefits | ₹1,500 |
| Incentives | ₹1,000 |
| Dialler and call software | ₹2,500 |
| CRM/helpdesk allocation | ₹800 |
| Google Workspace/email | ₹600 |
| Laptop depreciation/rental | ₹1,500 |
| Headset/equipment allocation | ₹300 |
| Internet and electricity | ₹1,000 |
| Office/rent allocation | ₹2,000 |
| Supervisor allocation | ₹2,000 |
| HR/recruitment allocation | ₹700 |
| Training allocation | ₹800 |
| Finance/admin/legal allocation | ₹800 |
| Marketing/sales allocation | ₹1,000 |
| Absence/bench/replacement provision | ₹1,500 |
| Contingency | ₹1,000 |
| **Illustrative full cost** | **₹34,000/month** |

These are example numbers, not final numbers.

Your actual sheet must calculate these from real values.

---

# 6. Productive capacity is not 100%

An employee may be paid for approximately 176 hours per month, but not every hour is billable.

Time is lost to:

- Training
- Meetings
- Breaks
- Reporting
- System problems
- Absence
- Internal communication
- Quality review
- Waiting time
- Unproductive or disconnected calls

Assume only **70%–80% productive utilisation** initially.

Example:

- Paid hours: 176
- Productive utilisation: 75%
- Productive hours: 132

If the fully loaded monthly cost is ₹34,000:

> Internal productive-hour cost = ₹34,000 ÷ 132 = approximately ₹258

After profit and business-risk provision, the selling rate might need to be ₹400–₹550 per productive hour, depending on the service.

---

# 7. Use a pricing formula

## Dedicated-agent pricing

> **Monthly client price = fully loaded cost ÷ target cost percentage**

Suppose:

- Full employee cost = ₹34,000
- You want cost to remain at 70% of revenue

Then:

> ₹34,000 ÷ 0.70 = ₹48,571

Round and sell at approximately:

- ₹49,000
- ₹52,000
- ₹55,000

depending on complexity and SLA.

## Why this matters

Charging ₹30,000 because the employee salary is ₹15,000 is dangerous.

The remaining ₹15,000 must cover:

- Software
- Telephone calls
- Supervision
- Training
- Equipment
- Administration
- Absence
- Sales cost
- Tax/compliance
- Profit
- Future growth

---

# 8. Create three service levels

Do not create ten confusing packages.

Use three standard levels across the company.

## Essential

For small businesses testing outsourcing.

- Shared team
- Defined working hours
- Basic script/process
- Standard reporting
- Limited channels
- Monthly volume cap
- Standard support response

## Growth

For businesses needing regular operations.

- Dedicated or semi-dedicated resource
- Calls plus CRM updates
- WhatsApp/email integration
- Supervisor review
- Weekly performance report
- Custom scripts
- Basic quality monitoring

## Managed

For clients outsourcing an entire function.

- Dedicated team
- Team leader/supervisor
- Voice and non-voice channels
- CRM/helpdesk operation
- Quality monitoring
- Workforce planning
- Client dashboard
- SLA
- Escalation management
- Monthly business review

---

# 9. Example product packages

These are **initial business-model ranges**, not final public prices.

## A. Customer Support Desk

| Package | Indicative structure | Indicative price |
|---|---|---:|
| Essential Shared Desk | Up to agreed ticket/call volume; shared agents | ₹15,000–₹30,000/month |
| Growth Support Agent | One dedicated daytime agent | ₹45,000–₹65,000/month |
| Managed Support Desk | 3–5 agents, supervisor allocation, reporting and QA | ₹1.8–₹3.5 lakh/month |
| Enterprise | Custom staffing, SLA, shifts and omnichannel | Custom quotation |

## B. Outbound Growth Desk

| Package | Indicative structure | Indicative price |
|---|---|---:|
| Pilot Campaign | Fixed lead volume or 2–4 week pilot | ₹25,000–₹60,000 |
| Dedicated Caller | One agent, scripts, CRM and reporting | ₹45,000–₹65,000/month |
| Appointment Desk | Base retainer plus qualified appointment fee | ₹30,000–₹50,000 plus incentive |
| Managed Campaign Team | 5 agents plus supervision and reporting | ₹2.5–₹4 lakh/month |

## C. Recruitment Support

| Package | Indicative structure | Indicative price |
|---|---|---:|
| Candidate Screening | Per screened candidate | ₹150–₹500/candidate |
| Interview Coordination | Screening and scheduling | ₹300–₹800/candidate |
| Monthly Recruitment Desk | Dedicated recruitment support executive | ₹45,000–₹70,000/month |
| Recruitment Campaign | Fixed role/candidate target | Custom quotation |

## D. Back-Office Operations

| Package | Billing basis | Indicative range |
|---|---|---:|
| Data processing | Per record | ₹3–₹30+ per record |
| Document validation | Per document | ₹10–₹100+ |
| Product/catalogue upload | Per SKU | ₹10–₹100+ |
| Dedicated back-office executive | Monthly | ₹40,000–₹60,000 |
| Managed transaction desk | Volume commitment | Custom |

The final figure depends on complexity, accuracy requirement and turnaround time.

---

# 10. International-client pricing must be separate

Do not convert Indian pricing directly into pounds or dollars.

International clients normally require:

- International phone numbers
- More expensive calling
- Night shifts
- UK/US compliance
- Stronger English communication
- Data-processing agreements
- Higher information-security controls
- Higher insurance/compliance expectations
- Currency and payment risk
- More detailed reporting
- Client-specific onboarding

Therefore, maintain separate rate cards.

| Delivery type | Possible monthly price per dedicated agent |
|---|---:|
| India domestic basic process | ₹45,000–₹60,000 |
| India specialised/omnichannel process | ₹60,000–₹90,000 |
| UK client standard support | £900–£1,500 |
| UK specialised/managed service | £1,500–£2,500+ |
| US standard support | US$1,200–US$2,000 |
| US specialised/managed service | US$2,000–US$3,500+ |

These are planning bands. The final price must come from your cost model and client requirements.

---

# 11. Separate setup fee from monthly fee

A client creates work before the first call is made.

You may need to:

- Understand the business
- Design the process
- Prepare scripts
- Configure the dialler
- Configure CRM fields
- Import data
- Train employees
- Create reports
- Test calls
- Create escalation rules
- Set up client access

Therefore charge:

## One-time setup fee

| Client complexity | Indicative setup fee |
|---|---:|
| Simple shared service | ₹10,000–₹25,000 |
| Dedicated agent/process | ₹25,000–₹75,000 |
| Multi-channel team | ₹75,000–₹2,50,000 |
| Enterprise migration/integration | Custom |

Do not give all implementation work free simply to win the client.

---

# 12. Add-ons must be priced separately

| Add-on | Charging method |
|---|---|
| Additional calling minutes | Actual usage plus margin |
| International number | Monthly rental plus setup |
| WhatsApp Business | Platform fee plus Meta usage charges |
| CRM licence | Per user/month |
| Custom dashboard | One-time plus support |
| Additional language | Per agent or premium percentage |
| Weekend/night shift | 15%–40% premium |
| Dedicated supervisor | Monthly fixed fee |
| Speech analytics | Per user/minute/month |
| Call-recording retention | Storage charge |
| Custom integration | Project fee |
| Client-site visit | Per-day fee plus travel |
| Urgent campaign launch | Expedited setup premium |

This prevents the base package from carrying unlimited hidden obligations.

---

# 13. How the website service page should look

Do not show a massive vendor-style technical matrix on the customer-facing page.

Use this structure:

## Product name

**Outbound Growth Desk**

### One-line promise

> Turn your lead database into qualified conversations, appointments and measurable follow-ups.

### Suitable for

- Real estate
- Education
- Healthcare
- Financial services
- Home services
- B2B companies

### What we do

- Call assigned leads
- Follow an approved script
- Qualify prospects
- Update CRM
- Schedule appointments
- Conduct follow-ups
- Submit daily and weekly reports

### Packages

| Essential | Growth | Managed |
|---|---|---|
| Shared calling team | Dedicated caller | Dedicated campaign team |
| Standard script | Custom script | Custom process and SLA |
| Basic report | CRM updates | Dashboard and QA |
| Defined volume | Monthly capacity | Full campaign management |
| From ₹X | From ₹Y | Custom quotation |

### Measured results

- Calls attempted
- Calls connected
- Interested prospects
- Qualified leads
- Appointments booked
- Follow-ups pending
- Conversion contribution

### Call to action

> Start with a two-week pilot.

---

# 14. Do not publish every price immediately

Use three price-visibility levels.

| Product | Website pricing approach |
|---|---|
| Simple standardised service | Publish “Starting from” |
| Moderately variable service | Publish package structure and starting range |
| Complex managed service | “Custom quotation” |
| Enterprise/multi-country | Discovery and proposal only |

Example:

> Dedicated India-based outbound calling support from ₹49,000 per month, subject to campaign scope, call volume and integrations.

This gives clarity without locking the company into an incorrect price.

---

# 15. Business growth plan

## Phase 1 — Validate: 0–5 active clients

### Objective

Prove one or two repeatable services.

### Products

- Outbound Growth Desk
- Recruitment Support Desk
- Shared Customer Support Desk

### Operating model

- 3–5 agents
- One working dialler
- One CRM
- Standard scripts
- Standard reporting
- Founder-led sales
- Four-week client pilots

### Do not do yet

- Purchase an expensive enterprise platform
- Launch every service category
- Recruit 50 people
- Promise 24/7 support
- Build custom software
- Accept heavily regulated work without readiness

---

## Phase 2 — Standardise: 5–15 active clients

### Add

- Operations manager/team leader
- QA scorecard
- Training library
- Formal client onboarding
- Standard SLAs
- Service catalogue
- Three-tier packages
- Monthly recurring revenue tracking
- Profit by client
- Employee utilisation reporting

---

## Phase 3 — Scale: 15–40 active clients

### Add

- Multiple team leaders
- Dedicated sales function
- HR/recruitment team
- Workforce management
- Stronger contact-centre platform
- Client dashboards
- Security policies
- Account management
- Process audit
- Specialised service verticals

---

## Phase 4 — Enterprise: 40+ clients

### Add

- Multi-tenant contact-centre architecture
- Compliance leadership
- 24/7/NOC-style operations where justified
- ISO/security certification roadmap
- Enterprise contracts
- International delivery
- Dedicated client pods
- Business continuity and disaster recovery
- Strong financial controls

Your earlier discussion mentioned ambitions such as 75 clients and 100 partners. That is a direction, but it must be reached through staged capacity, documentation and ownership—not through immediate recruitment or software purchasing. fileciteturn0file0

---

# 16. Which dialler should you buy now?

The dialler decision should be based on the first product and first five clients.

## For a 3–10 agent India pilot

Shortlist:

- Knowlarity
- Tata Smartflo
- Exotel

Evaluate:

- Connection quality
- Caller-ID/spam experience
- Recording
- Reports
- Agent usability
- Supervisor functionality
- Actual monthly total
- Contract lock-in
- CRM integration

Do not purchase Ozonetel/Ameyo enterprise architecture before the real process requires it.

## Decision rule

Choose the platform that satisfies the first 12 months—not the imagined 500-agent company.

But confirm:

- Ability to migrate numbers/data
- Export recordings/reports
- API availability
- No unreasonable lock-in

---

# 17. What must be created now

The next work should produce six connected sheets.

| Sheet | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **01 Service Catalogue** | Every service, deliverable, unit and exclusion |
| **02 Cost Model** | Employee, software, telecom and overhead costs |
| **03 Pricing Calculator** | Dedicated agent, hour, call, transaction and campaign pricing |
| **04 Package Builder** | Essential, Growth and Managed packages |
| **05 Client Profitability** | Revenue, direct cost, gross margin and collection status |
| **06 Growth Capacity Plan** | Clients, employees, supervisors, software and cash requirement |

---

# 18. Immediate decision sequence

Follow this order:

### Decision 1: Select only three launch products

Recommended:

1. Outbound Growth Desk  
2. Recruitment Support Desk  
3. Shared Customer Support Desk  

### Decision 2: Calculate the true monthly cost of one agent

Use real salary, software, call and overhead values.

### Decision 3: Define minimum acceptable gross margin

Start with a target such as **30%–40% gross margin**, then test market feasibility.

### Decision 4: Create one package for each product

- Pilot
- Dedicated/Growth
- Managed

### Decision 5: Obtain dialler quotations using the same 5-agent and 20-agent scenario

### Decision 6: Test with one internal or friendly client

### Decision 7: Correct pricing using actual pilot data

### Decision 8: Publish the service pages

---

# Final direction

You do not currently need another giant software comparison.

You need this commercial chain:

> **Service → work unit → employee capacity → total cost → margin → price → package → website page → pilot → measured result → revised price**

At present, the most important missing item is the **full costing and pricing model**, not the final dialler selection.

Your initial business can be positioned as:

> **A managed customer engagement and business operations company providing voice, digital support, recruitment assistance and back-office execution through trained India-based teams.**

And your first commercial package can begin with:

> **Two-week pilot → one-month dedicated service → managed team expansion.**
