# 21. The planning view you actually need

A visual schedule is still useful, but it should contain several layers.

## View 1 — Demand heatmap

| Time | Client A voice | Client B chat | Client C WhatsApp | Total need |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 09:00–10:00 | 2 | 0 | 1 | 3 |
| 10:00–11:00 | 3 | 1 | 1 | 5 |
| 11:00–12:00 | 2 | 2 | 1 | 5 |

---

## View 2 — Workforce availability

| Time | Tamil voice | English voice | Chat | WhatsApp |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 09:00–10:00 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 2 |
| 10:00–11:00 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 1 |
| 11:00–12:00 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 2 |

---

## View 3 — Coverage gap

| Time | Required | Available | Gap |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 09:00–10:00 | 3 | 4 | +1 |
| 10:00–11:00 | 5 | 4 | **−1** |
| 11:00–12:00 | 5 | 4 | **−1** |

---

## View 4 — Allocation

This can look like a Gantt or rota:

| Worker | 09:00 | 10:00 | 11:00 | 12:00 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lakshmi | Client A | Client A | Break | Client C |
| Meena | Client C | Client B | Client B | Flexible |
| Kavitha | Backup | Client A | Client A | Client A |

So yes, a Gantt-style resource chart is one display, but the core engine is the demand-versus-capacity calculation behind it.

---

# 22. Daily control process

## Previous evening

- Workers confirm tomorrow’s blocks
- System recalculates coverage
- Gaps are flagged
- Backup workers are invited
- Low-priority flexible work is moved

## Start of day

- Confirm logins
- Compare scheduled versus actual
- Protect live-service queues first
- Allocate asynchronous work to remaining capacity

## During day

- Monitor call/chat/ticket volume
- Compare forecast against actual
- Reassign trained resources
- Inform client if an exceptional SLA risk arises

## End of day

- Record actual hours
- Record task outputs
- Compare planned versus actual availability
- Update reliability score
- Calculate worker TEE/TEM contribution
- Update future forecasts

Intraday management is exactly the discipline of comparing actual performance to the plan and correcting staffing during the operating day. citeturn136062search12turn136062search29

---

# 23. What “limited” now means

When I previously said some services have “no” or “limited” flexibility, the corrected interpretation is:

| Service | Can your model support it? | Condition |
|---|---:|---|
| Inbound phone | Yes | Pre-committed time blocks and backups |
| Live chat | Yes | Scheduled monitoring coverage |
| Emergency service | Later | Strong reserve pool and escalation owner |
| Complaint escalation | Yes | Case owner plus documented handover |
| 24×7 support | Yes, at scale | Sufficient workers across all time blocks |
| Foreign-language support | Yes, later | Certified language pool and training recovery |
| Highly variable demand | Yes | Forecasting, buffer and overage controls |

Therefore, these services are not prohibited.

They are **capacity-dependent products**.

---

# 24. Final operating principle

The business should use this order:

> **Collect worker availability → classify commitment → map skills → calculate reliable capacity → reserve backup → identify sellable service windows → acquire matching clients → allocate work → monitor daily adherence → measure cost and quality → increase capacity**

Not:

> Acquire any client → hope workers become available.

---

# 25. Immediate implementation decision

For the first pilot, build a 30-minute interval capacity sheet containing:

### Worker side

- Worker
- Date
- Start time
- End time
- Availability type
- Skill
- Language
- Client certification
- Confirmed status
- Actual attendance
- Reliability score
- Internal rate

### Client side

- Client
- Service
- Date
- Start time
- End time
- Forecast contacts
- Average handling time
- After-work time
- Required concurrency
- Language
- SLA
- Backup percentage
- Revenue

### Output

- Required minutes
- Available minutes
- Skill-adjusted capacity
- Gap/surplus
- Sellable remaining capacity
- Internal cost
- Proposed client price
- Expected gross margin

That sheet should run manually for the pilot. After actual operational data exists, it can be moved into Zoho Creator, Power Apps or a specialised WFM platform.

The correct description for the product you are building is:

> **A skill-based, availability-driven distributed workforce orchestration system for shared voice and non-voice business services.**

# 17/07/2026 20:42 — You're Describing an Asynchronous Inbound Model

Partner, now I understand your thinking much better.

When I said:

> "The employee needs to wait for the call"

I was describing a **traditional call-centre SLA model**.

Example:

```text
HSBC
Barclays
Amazon
Airtel
```

Customer calls.

Expectation:

```text
Answer within 20 sec
Answer within 30 sec
Answer within 60 sec
```

Therefore agents must sit and wait.

Because customer is expecting immediate service.

---

# What you are proposing is different

You are saying:

```text
Customer calls
↓
System receives call
↓
Collect details
↓
Create task
↓
Callback later
↓
Available employee handles it
```

This is completely valid.

In fact many modern companies are moving towards this model.

---

# There are actually 3 inbound models

## Model 1 — Traditional Live Queue

```text
Customer calls
↓
Wait in queue
↓
Agent answers immediately
```

Example:

- Bank fraud
- Credit card block
- Emergency support

Needs:

```text
Agent waiting
```

This is expensive.

---

## Model 2 — Callback Queue

```text
Customer calls
↓
IVR
↓
Leave details
↓
Task created
↓
Agent calls back
```

Example:

- Sales enquiry
- General enquiry
- Appointment booking
- Recruitment enquiry

Needs:

```text
No agent waiting
```

Much cheaper.

---

## Model 3 — Hybrid

```text
Press 1 → urgent
Press 2 → callback
```

Urgent:

```text
Live queue
```

Normal:

```text
Callback queue
```

Most scalable.

---

# This matches your workforce model

Your women workforce is perfect for:

```text
Callback queue
```

because:

Worker available

↓

Picks next task

↓

Calls customer

↓

Updates CRM

↓

Completes task

↓

Picks next task

No waiting.

---

# Let's take a dental clinic

Traditional BPO:

```text
Receptionist waiting
```

9am-6pm

Even if nobody calls.

---

Your model:

Patient calls.

IVR:

```text
Thank you.

Please tell us:

Name
Phone
Requirement
```

AI transcribes.

Task created.

```text
Appointment enquiry
```

Worker free at 11am.

Calls patient.

Problem solved.

---

# Huge Cost Difference

Traditional:

```text
Need 8 hrs coverage
```

Even if:

```text
30 mins actual work
```

---

Your model:

```text
Only pay for 30 mins work
```

This is why your model becomes economical.

---

# Where I still disagree

Some services cannot be callback-first.

Example:

Customer:

```text

```

Bank cannot say:


## Fraud

```text

```

Need immediate.

---

## Production outage

Corporate customer:

```text
System down.
```

Need immediate.

---

For these:

```text
Live queue required.
```

---

# Most SME clients don't need live queue

This is important.

Many SMEs can accept:

### Dental

```text
Callback within 30 mins
```

---

### Real Estate

```text
Callback within 15 mins
```

---

### Training Institute

```text
Callback within 1 hour
```

---

### Recruitment

```text
Callback within 4 hours
```

---

### Insurance Lead

```text
Callback within 30 mins
```

---

These fit your model.

Don't sell:

```text
Inbound Support
```

Sell:

```text
Response SLA
```

Example:

## Bronze

```text
Callback within 4 hrs
```

---

## Silver

```text
Callback within 1 hr
```

---

## Gold

```text
Callback within 15 mins
```

---

```text
Live Answer
```

Customer calls.

```text
Exotel
Knowlarity
Smartflo
```

IVR answers.

Options:

```text
1 Sales
2 Support
3 Appointment
4 Leave Message
```

Speech-to-text.

AI summary.

Creates ticket.

```text
Zoho Desk
```

or

```text
CRM
```

Calls customer.

This fits your shared workforce perfectly.

Earlier I said:

```text
Need 720 hours coverage
```

for 24×7.

```text
Live answering
```

Need:

```text
Forecasted work minutes
```

not

```text
Coverage hours
```

200 calls/day

```text
5 mins
```

Workload:

```text
1000 mins/day
```

---

# Your likely sweet spot

For first 3 years I would focus on:

### Callback Sales

- Lead qualification
- Appointment booking
- Real estate

### Recruitment

- Candidate screening
- Interview scheduling

### Healthcare

- Appointment booking
- Follow-up

### Education

- Admission enquiries

### SME Customer Service

- Callback within SLA

### WhatsApp Desk

- Response within SLA

These fit your distributed women-workforce model extremely well.

---

# What I would avoid initially

### 24×7 live banking

### Fraud support

### Emergency medical

### Telecom outage desks

### Mission-critical corporate support

Because these require:

```text
Guaranteed real-time staffing
```

and your workforce model is currently optimized for:

```text
Task queue + callback + SLA response
```

not continuous live-answer operations.

So your challenge is not really:

> "How many people must wait for calls?"

Your challenge is:

> "How many callback tasks, WhatsApp conversations, emails, tickets and appointments will arrive, and how many certified worker-minutes do I have available to complete them within the promised SLA?"

That is a much better fit for the social-enterprise model you're designing.

### 0044 · CHATGPT · 2026-07-17T19:06:34Z

# 17/07/2026 20:55 — You Are Moving Towards an Operations Control Tower

Partner, now we are no longer discussing a BPO.

We are discussing something closer to:

> **Workforce Orchestration + Service Management + Decision Engine + AI Assisted Operations**

And I think you've identified the next major design principle correctly:

> Not all calls are equal.

A stolen credit card call and a product enquiry call cannot enter the same queue with the same priority.

---

# 1. Traditional BPO thinking vs Your thinking

Traditional BPO:

```text
Queue A
  ↓
Agent A

Queue B
  ↓
Agent B

Queue C
  ↓
Agent C
```

Each agent belongs to a client.

---

Your model:

```text
Client A
Client B
Client C
Client D
      ↓
Unified Work Pool
      ↓
Priority Engine
      ↓
Available Workforce
```

Workers belong to the workforce pool.

Not to a client.

This is a major difference.

---

# 2. The real unit is NOT a call

This is important.

You are thinking:

> Call

Actually the system should think:

> Work Item

A work item can be:

```text
Inbound call
Outbound call
WhatsApp
Email
Live chat
Complaint
Fraud
Appointment
Candidate screening
```

Everything becomes:

```text
Work Item
```

---

# 3. Every work item needs a priority score

Instead of:

```text
High
Medium
Low
```

Use a score.

Example:

```text
0 - 1000
```

---

## Example

### Medical Emergency

```text
Priority = 1000
```

---

### Fraud

```text
Priority = 950
```

---

### System Outage

```text
Priority = 900
```

---

### Customer Complaint

```text
Priority = 700
```

---

### Appointment Booking

```text
Priority = 500
```

---

### Sales Enquiry

```text
Priority = 300
```

---

### Marketing Follow-up

```text
Priority = 100
```

Now system can sort automatically.

---

# 4. Who decides priority?

You asked:

> Customer thinks everything is urgent.

Correct.

Customer cannot decide.

The system decides.

---

Example:

Customer says:

```text
My parcel is delayed.
```

Customer:

```text
URGENT !!!
```

System:

```text
Complaint
Priority 450
```

---

Customer says:

```text
Someone is using my card.
```

System:

```text
Fraud
Priority 950
```

---

Customer says:

```text
Patient collapsed.
```

System:

```text
Medical
Priority 1000
```

---

# 5. How does system determine priority?

Three layers.

---

## Layer 1

Rule Engine

Example:

```text
IF
Keyword = fraud

THEN
Priority = 950
```

---

```text
IF
Keyword = payment failed

Priority = 700
```

---

```text
IF
Keyword = appointment

Priority = 300
```

---

Very cheap.

Very reliable.

Very explainable.

---

## Layer 2

AI Classification

When rules fail.

Customer says:

```text
I think somebody hacked my account.
```

Rule may not find exact keyword.

AI understands.

Returns:

```text
Fraud Related
Confidence 85%
Priority 920
```

---

## Layer 3

Human Override

Supervisor sees:

```text
Priority 400
```

But knows customer is CEO.

Changes:

```text
Priority 800
```

Human always wins.

---

# 6. Your knowledge-base idea is correct

You mentioned:

> Collect knowledge
> Store it
> Use AI only when required

This is exactly right.

Do NOT make AI decide everything.

---

Better model:

```text
Knowledge Table
```

Example:

| Situation | Priority |
|------------|-----------|
| Fraud | 950 |
| Medical | 1000 |
| Lost Card | 900 |
| Complaint | 500 |
| Sales | 300 |

---

AI only helps when:

```text
Unknown Situation
```

appears.

---

Then:

```text
AI Suggests
Human Reviews
Knowledge Updated
```

---

This becomes:

```text
Learning System
```

---

# 7. Emergency Capacity Buffer

This is the biggest thing you just identified.

Traditional workforce planning:

```text
Capacity = Demand
```

Bad.

---

Need:

```text
Capacity = Demand + Reserve
```

---

Example

Need:

```text
100 hrs
```

---

Don't sell:

```text
100 hrs
```

---

Sell:

```text
80 hrs
```

---

Keep:

```text
20 hrs reserve
```

---

Same as:

```text
Cloud Servers
Hospitals
Airlines
Banks
```

all do.

---

# 8. Workforce States

Every worker should have a status.

---

### Available

```text
Can accept work
```

---

### Busy

```text
Currently handling work
```

---

### Reserved

```text
Emergency buffer
```

---

### Training

```text
Learning
```

---

### Break

```text
Unavailable
```

---

### Offline

```text
Not working
```

---

Then system knows actual capacity.

---

# 9. What happens if urgent work arrives?

Example:

Worker doing:

```text
Sales Follow-up
Priority 200
```

---

New work arrives:

```text
Fraud Alert
Priority 950
```

System:

```text
Check Available Workers
```

---

If available:

```text
Assign immediately
```

---

If none available:

Check:

```text
Reserved Workers
```

---

If reserve available:

```text
Activate Reserve
```

---

Only if no reserve exists:

```text
Interrupt lower priority work
```

---

This is exactly how operations centres work.

---

# 10. Work Preemption Matrix

Create a table.

| Current Task | Can Interrupt? |
|--------------|---------------|
| Marketing Call | Yes |
| Sales Follow-up | Yes |
| Appointment Booking | Usually |
| Complaint Investigation | Sometimes |
| Fraud Case | No |
| Medical Emergency | No |

Now system knows what can be paused.

---

# 11. Your future architecture

I would design:

```text
Client
    ↓
Work Item Created
    ↓
Priority Engine
    ↓
Knowledge Base
    ↓
AI Classification
    ↓
Human Validation
    ↓
Queue Manager
    ↓
Workforce Allocation
    ↓
Execution
    ↓
QA
    ↓
Learning Database
```

---

# 12. What software supports this today?

No single low-cost product does all of this.

You are describing something between:

### Contact Centre WFM

and

### ServiceNow

and

### Jira Service Management

and

### Workforce Marketplace

and

### AI Copilot

---

For pilot stage:

```text
Zoho Desk
+
Zoho CRM
+
Zoho Shifts
+
Exotel
+
Zoho Creator
```

can achieve perhaps **70–80%** of what you want.

---

# 13. The bigger realization

Initially we were discussing:

> How many calls can workers make?

Now the actual question is:

> How many prioritized work items can the workforce process while maintaining SLA, quality, training requirements, emergency reserves and profitability?

That is a completely different optimisation problem.

And that is why I increasingly see your model not as:

> Women BPO

but as:

> **AUMNITY Workforce Orchestration Platform**
>
> A shared skilled workforce where:
> - people contribute available capacity,
> - work is prioritized automatically,
> - knowledge is stored centrally,
> - AI assists classification,
> - humans remain in control,
> - and clients buy outcomes, response windows and service capacity rather than named employees.

That architecture is much closer to what you are describing than a traditional BPO or telecalling company.

### 0049 · CHATGPT · 2026-07-17T19:14:29Z

# 17/07/2026 21:18 — Integrated Social-Enterprise BPO Model

Partner, after reviewing the full discussion, the business is no longer adequately described as a normal BPO, call centre or staffing company.

The coherent model is:

> **A technology-enabled social enterprise that converts client operations into prioritised work items, distributes them across a trained home-based women workforce according to availability, skill, language and urgency, preserves client knowledge centrally, and charges clients for service capacity, transactions, response commitments and outcomes.**

The earlier discussion already established the essential building blocks: flexible home workers, client-independent resource pooling, weekly availability, daily confirmation, recording, centralised knowledge, unit-based pricing, emergency prioritisation, human-in-the-loop decisions and the separation of internal-company software from client-service software. fileciteturn0file0

---

# 1. The complete business model

## 1.1 What the company sells

The company should not sell “a telecaller” or “one employee.”

It should sell five types of commercial products:

| Product type | What the client buys | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Transaction capacity** | A defined number of completed work units | 1,000 order confirmations |
| **Productive-time capacity** | A pool of usable service minutes or hours | 100 support hours |
| **Response SLA** | A guaranteed response within a defined period | Callback within 30 minutes |
| **Coverage window** | Availability during agreed time blocks | WhatsApp desk, 9am–1pm |
| **Outcome package** | A base service plus measurable result incentive | Qualification fee plus appointment bonus |

The client does not buy Lakshmi, Meena or a named worker. The client buys a measurable service commitment.

---

## 1.2 What workers contribute

Each woman contributes one or more of the following:

- Flexible asynchronous time
- Confirmed weekly capacity
- Committed live-service blocks
- Voice skills
- Non-voice skills
- Client-specific certification
- Language skills
- Domain expertise
- Emergency-reserve availability

The system combines small contributions into pooled commercial capacity.

---

## 1.3 What the company retains

The company must retain ownership of:

- Client relationship
- Process design
- Knowledge base
- Customer history
- Quality standards
- Work allocation
- Pricing
- Technology
- Security
- Reports
- Escalation control

This prevents the service from becoming an unmanaged freelancer marketplace.

---

# 2. The operating model

Every client interaction becomes a **work item**.

## Work-item lifecycle

```text
Client/customer contact
        ↓
Call, WhatsApp, email, form or uploaded batch
        ↓
Identity and client recognised
        ↓
Intent classified
        ↓
Urgency and SLA calculated
        ↓
Work item created
        ↓
Matched against skill, language and availability
        ↓
Assigned to available or reserved worker
        ↓
Worker receives client context and history
        ↓
Action completed
        ↓
Mandatory notes and evidence captured
        ↓
Quality check
        ↓
Client report and worker payment calculation
```

The central business asset is therefore not the dialler alone.

It is the combination of:

> **Work intake + priority engine + knowledge system + task queue + workforce allocation + quality control + billing.**

---

# 3. Priority and urgency model

Your idea of interrupting a low-priority outbound activity when a genuinely urgent inbound request arrives is operationally valid, but it requires rules.

## Suggested priority classes

| Priority | Example | Target handling |
|---|---|---|
| **P0 Critical** | Immediate danger to life, active fraud, security breach | Immediate specialist escalation |
| **P1 Urgent** | Account blocked, transaction failure, major service outage | Immediate or within 5–15 minutes |
| **P2 High** | Time-sensitive complaint, delivery failure before event | 15–30 minutes |
| **P3 Standard** | Appointment, order query, normal complaint | 1–4 hours |
| **P4 Planned** | Sales enquiry, lead qualification, follow-up | Same day/agreed window |
| **P5 Batch** | Data entry, catalogue updates, research | Deadline-based |

## Who decides?

Not the customer alone.

The final classification should combine:

1. Client-approved rule table  
2. IVR or form answers  
3. Keywords and structured data  
4. AI recommendation  
5. Human override  

## Correct AI use

AI should:

- Transcribe
- Summarise
- Detect intent
- Recommend priority
- Extract entities
- Suggest the next workflow

AI should **not independently make irreversible medical, financial, fraud or safety decisions**.

The system should store:

- AI classification
- Confidence
- Rule matched
- Human decision
- Override reason
- Final outcome

Unknown cases can later be reviewed and added to the controlled rule table.

---

# 4. The two software estates

You correctly identified that there are two different software groups.

## Estate A — Software required to run your company

This remains your internal operating infrastructure regardless of which client you serve.

## Estate B — Software required to deliver a particular client service

This varies by client, service, channel, SLA and integration requirement.

The two should be priced separately in the business model.

---

# 5. Estate A: Internal company software

## 5.1 Company identity and collaboration

### Preferred option: Google Workspace

Use for:

- Company email
- Shared Drive
- Docs and policies
- Training material
- Meet
- Calendar
- Forms
- Internal spreadsheets

Google Workspace is appropriate for collaboration, but Google Voice is a separate telephony product. Google’s current official supported-country list for Google Voice includes the UK, US and several European countries, but not India. citeturn584749search4

### Why Google Voice is not in India

It is not because India lacks a BPO market. India has one of the world’s largest technology and BPM industries: NASSCOM reported BPM revenues of about US$44 billion in FY22, while India’s overall technology sector is expected to exceed US$315 billion in FY26. citeturn234312search20turn234312search24

The more reasonable explanation is a combination of:

- Indian telecom licensing and carrier integration
- Emergency-calling obligations
- Number allocation and local routing
- Lawful interception and record requirements
- Domestic PSTN/VoIP rules
- Commercial product priorities

That is an inference; Google has not publicly stated one single reason explaining India’s exclusion.

---

## 5.2 HR, worker profile and attendance

### Recommended pilot option: Zoho People

Needed for:

- Worker master data
- Documents
- Leave
- Attendance
- Skills
- Timesheets
- Client/project time mapping
- Shift records
- Training history

Zoho People supports shift creation, scheduling, timesheets, projects/clients mapping and job scheduling. citeturn981991search7

### Additional scheduling layer

Use either:

- Zoho People/Shifts for early operation
- Deputy/Connecteam for stronger rota-style operations
- Enterprise WFM later

---

## 5.3 Capacity and workforce orchestration

No basic HR package fully solves your model.

You need a capacity engine that maintains:

### Worker supply

- Availability by 15/30/60-minute blocks
- Confirmed versus preferred availability
- Skills
- Languages
- Client certification
- Reliability
- QA score
- Current state
- Cost rate

### Client demand

- Service
- Contact forecast
- Average handling time
- After-work time
- Priority distribution
- SLA
- Required language
- Backup percentage
- Revenue rate

### Outputs

- Required capacity
- Available capacity
- Reserved emergency capacity
- Skill-adjusted capacity
- Gap
- Sellable surplus
- Cost
- Revenue
- Margin

### Pilot implementation

Use:

- Google Sheets or Zoho Analytics for calculation
- Zoho Creator for a low-code front end
- Zoho People/Shifts as the worker-availability source

### Later

Build a custom orchestration application only after real pilot data exists.

---

## 5.4 Internal task and process management

Use:

- Zoho Projects for internal projects
- Zoho Qntrl for controlled workflows
- Zoho Creator for custom work-item orchestration
- ClickUp as an alternative

This layer handles:

- Client onboarding
- Training
- Process changes
- Technology setup
- Quality actions
- Internal responsibilities

It should not replace the customer-service ticket history.

---

## 5.5 Training and certification

Initially:

- Google Classroom or structured Shared Drive
- Google Forms tests
- Recorded mock calls
- Certification matrix in Zoho People/Creator

Later:

- Zoho Learn
- TalentLMS
- Dedicated learning-management system

Each worker should be certified by:

- Channel
- Language
- Client
- Process
- Priority level
- Data sensitivity

A worker trained only for normal sales calls must not automatically receive fraud, medical or financial tasks.

---

## 5.6 Finance and worker payment

Use:

- Zoho Books for accounting and invoicing
- Zoho Payroll where applicable
- Custom calculation sheet/Creator app for flexible worker payments

The worker-payment engine needs:

- Committed availability payment
- Active work time
- Transaction incentive
- Skill premium
- Quality bonus
- Reliability bonus
- Training payment
- Expense reimbursement

---

## 5.7 Security

Internal requirements include:

- Role-based access
- Password manager
- Device register
- MFA
- Restricted downloads
- Client separation
- Recording access controls
- Audit history
- Worker exit process

India’s revised OSP rules permit work-from-home/work-from-anywhere remote agents using wireline or wireless broadband, but they retain conditions around remote-agent operation and logs. citeturn234312search21

The 2020 rules also required tamper-resistant system logs and retention of call/data records and activity logs for one year in specified OSP circumstances. Legal applicability should be checked for the exact service design. citeturn234312search1

---

# 6. Estate B: Client-service software

## 6.1 Telephony and dialler

### Pilot shortlist

| Product | Best fit |
|---|---|
| **Tata Smartflo** | India domestic voice, concurrency-based pricing |
| **Exotel** | APIs, voice, omnichannel and custom workflows |
| **Knowlarity** | Faster SME contact-centre launch |
| **Ozonetel** | More mature contact-centre and enterprise operations |
| **Ameyo** | Complex enterprise omnichannel/contact centre |

### Strong pilot candidate: Tata Smartflo

Published current options include:

| Product | Published cost |
|---|---:|
| ITS | ₹1,000 per licence/month + ₹10,000 setup |
| Outbound Dialler Unlimited | ₹1,200 per concurrency/month + ₹10,000 setup |
| Voice Streaming Inbound/Outbound | ₹1,100 per concurrency/month + ₹20,000 setup |
| Usage-based API platform | ₹25,000/₹1 lakh/₹5 lakh monthly tiers + setup |

The advertised unlimited offer is subject to a pooled fair-usage allowance of 5,000 minutes per user monthly. citeturn981991search6turn981991search18

For your model, concurrency is potentially more economical than buying a licence for every registered woman.

---

## 6.2 CRM

Use Zoho CRM for:

- Outbound sales
- Lead qualification
- Follow-up
- Appointment booking
- Recruitment pipeline
- Collections workflow
- Sales outcome tracking

Create client-specific pipelines and permissions.

---

## 6.3 Helpdesk and customer timeline

Use Zoho Desk for:

- Email tickets
- Complaints
- Customer history
- Escalations
- Knowledge base
- Assignment
- SLA
- Internal notes
- Multi-department handling

Zoho Desk can be analysed using Zoho Analytics for ticket inflow, satisfaction and agent-performance reporting. citeturn981991search11

This is where one worker can understand what another worker did previously.

---

## 6.4 WhatsApp and messaging

Use:

- Official WhatsApp Business Platform
- Shared inbox
- Client-specific number
- Assignment
- Conversation history
- Templates
- Escalation

Possible implementation:

- Exotel/Ameyo
- Zoho Desk messaging
- WATI
- Interakt
- Gallabox
- Airtel IQ

Do not use employees’ personal WhatsApp accounts.

---

## 6.5 Voice recording, transcription and AI

The client-service system should support:

- Call recording
- Speech-to-text
- Summary
- Intent extraction
- Priority recommendation
- PII masking where required
- Search
- Quality sampling
- Evidence retention

India’s multilingual speech environment remains technically challenging; a 2026 benchmark covered 15 major Indian languages across 139 regional clusters and highlighted variation in real-world telephonic speech. citeturn584749academia47

Therefore, AI transcription cannot be treated as perfectly accurate, particularly for:

- Tamil-English code switching
- Regional dialects
- Noisy home environments
- Product names
- Addresses
- Financial details

Human verification is mandatory for critical fields.

---

## 6.6 Client-specific applications

Some clients will already have:

- Salesforce
- Freshdesk
- SAP
- Hospital software
- E-commerce platform
- Order-management system
- Banking CRM
- Recruitment ATS

Three possible models:

### Model 1 — Use your standard platform

The client uses your Zoho/telephony environment.

Charge:

- Platform fee
- Configuration
- User/concurrency
- Usage
- Support

### Model 2 — Use client’s platform

Workers log into the client’s system.

Charge:

- Training
- Integration/onboarding
- Extra security
- Licence if client does not provide it
- Increased operating complexity

### Model 3 — Hybrid

Your orchestration layer controls workforce and priority, but customer records remain in the client’s system.

This is likely for larger corporates.

---

# 7. Can current software deliver the full concept?

## Coverage matrix

| Requirement | Existing software coverage | Remaining gap |
|---|---:|---|
| Company email/docs | 100% | None |
| HR records | 90% | Custom social-impact fields |
| Weekly availability | 85% | Reliability and client-capacity linkage |
| Shift scheduling | 85% | Multi-client dynamic orchestration |
| Dialling and recording | 90% | Cross-client priority interruption |
| CRM | 90% | Unified work item across channels |
| Customer ticket history | 90% | Capacity/payment linkage |
| WhatsApp shared inbox | 90% | Cross-client urgency scoring |
| Work-item priority | 50% | Custom rule/AI engine |
| Emergency buffer | 30% | Custom capacity logic |
| Multi-client skill matching | 50% | Custom orchestration |
| Sellable-capacity calculation | 20% | Must be built |
| Client-acquisition capacity gate | 10% | Must be built |
| Worker TEE-to-TEM payment | 20% | Must be built |
| Unified margin per work item | 20% | Must be built |
| Human-reviewed AI learning | 40% | Custom workflow/data model |
| Cross-channel pre-emption | 30% | Custom rules/integration |

## Conclusion

Existing software can provide approximately **70% of the operational components**.

The unique 30% is your real product innovation:

- Unified prioritised work pool
- Availability-driven allocation
- Shared multi-client worker model
- Emergency reserve
- Skill/language/client certification matching
- Worker earnings logic
- Sellable-capacity control
- Human-reviewed learning loop

# 9. How large Indian companies actually operate

## They usually do not depend on Google Voice

Large Indian companies typically use one or more of:

- Carrier-provided telephony
- Enterprise contact-centre platform
- Cloud communications APIs
- CRM/helpdesk
- In-house orchestration and business applications
- Outsourced BPO partner infrastructure

## Ekart / Flipkart ecosystem

Exotel publicly documents that Ekart, Flipkart’s logistics subsidiary, uses Exotel to connect delivery personnel and customers while protecting phone-number privacy. citeturn981991search12

That does **not prove that every Flipkart support call uses Exotel**. A company of Flipkart’s scale is likely to use multiple systems for:

- Delivery communications
- Customer support
- Seller support
- Fraud
- Logistics
- Internal operations

Exact full-stack architecture is generally commercially confidential.

---

## BigBasket

Ozonetel publicly states that it helped BigBasket achieve a 95% call-answer rate within ten seconds. citeturn981991search5

This demonstrates that large Indian e-commerce companies can use Indian contact-centre vendors rather than developing every telephony component themselves.

---

## Banks

Banks typically combine:

- Core banking system
- CRM
- Contact-centre platform
- IVR
- Authentication
- Fraud systems
- Knowledge management
- Workforce management
- Outsourced agents
- Custom integration

Enterprise platforms such as Genesys actively target India’s BFSI market, and cloud/contact-centre vendors emphasise security, orchestration and AI for Indian financial institutions. citeturn584749search1turn584749search20

The bank normally does not build every dialler component from zero. It buys platforms and builds proprietary integrations, rules and customer journeys around them.

---

## Delivery and logistics companies

They commonly need:

- Number masking
- Automated delivery calls
- Driver-customer connection
- OTP
- IVR
- Failed-delivery follow-up
- Call recordings
- Workflow APIs

This is a strong Exotel/Airtel IQ/Tata Smartflo-style use case rather than a Google Voice use case.

---

## Amazon

Amazon has extensive proprietary customer and logistics systems globally, but it also uses commercial telecom, carrier and technology components. The exact India contact-centre stack is not publicly documented sufficiently to state one specific dialler confidently.

The safe conclusion is:

> Large companies commonly combine purchased infrastructure with custom software; “in-house” usually means the orchestration and business logic are internal, not that every telephone-switching component was developed internally.

---

# 11. Existing comparable models

Your concept intersects with:

- Rural BPO
- Impact sourcing
- Work-from-home contact centres
- Shared service desks
- Microtask platforms
- Virtual assistant services
- Gig workforce platforms

India already has impact-sourcing and rural-BPO examples that combine commercial service delivery with employment creation. Research and case material describe women as a substantial part of the rural-BPO workforce, while the model’s recurring challenges include scale, infrastructure and reliable delivery. citeturn234312search6turn234312search10

What remains unusual in your model is:

> **Very flexible part-time supply combined with real-time multi-client prioritisation and a unified work queue.**

That combination is your opportunity and your operational risk.

# 17. The decisive conclusion

You are not trying to beat Flipkart, Amazon or a bank by constructing an enterprise contact centre immediately.

You are creating a new operating layer for clients that are too small to build those systems themselves.

Your target customer is initially:

> **An Indian SME that needs professional customer engagement or back-office execution but cannot afford a dedicated contact-centre team.**

Your competitive promise is:

> **Enterprise-style process discipline delivered through flexible shared capacity.**

The software strategy should therefore be:

> **Buy the proven building blocks; configure the workflows; build only the unique orchestration layer.**

The unique product is not the dialler.

It is:

> **A multi-client, priority-driven, skill-aware and availability-aware workforce orchestration platform built around home-based women professionals.**

### 0054 · CHATGPT · 2026-07-17T19:18:41Z

# 17/07/2026 21:42 — Tamil Transcription & Dialler Clarification

Partner, there are **two completely separate technology layers** causing the confusion:

1. **Speech-to-text:** converting Tamil or Tamil-English audio into text.
2. **Contact-centre platform:** receiving calls, dialling customers, recording conversations, creating tasks and routing work.

OpenAI, Claude and Perplexity are primarily AI-assistant/model companies. Amazon Connect, Dynamics 365 Contact Center, Exotel and Ozonetel are contact-centre systems. A contact-centre platform may use a separate speech-to-text engine internally.

---

# Part A — Tamil audio transcription

## 1. Chat application versus transcription API

These are not the same thing:

| Capability | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Microphone inside ChatGPT | The application captures speech and converts it into a message |
| Uploading an audio file | The application accepts an existing recording |
| Speech-to-text API | Your own program sends audio and receives a transcript |
| LLM processing | AI summarises, corrects or analyses the transcript after transcription |
| Contact-centre transcription | Calls are recorded and automatically transcribed as part of the workflow |

Claude or Perplexity accepting an audio file in a particular application does not automatically mean they provide a dedicated, commercially priced speech-to-text API.

---

# 2. Current API comparison

## Verified public pricing

| Provider | Dedicated STT API? | Tamil/Indian-language positioning | Batch/streaming | Current public price |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| **Sarvam AI** | Yes | Specifically designed for 22 Indian languages, code-mixing and telephony | REST, batch and streaming | **₹30/hour = ₹0.50/minute** |
| **Sarvam + diarisation** | Yes | Adds speaker identification | Batch | **₹45/hour = ₹0.75/minute** |
| **Sarvam transcription + English translation** | Yes | Indian-language speech translated to English | API | **₹30/hour = ₹0.50/minute** |
| **OpenAI streaming speech-to-text** | Yes | General multilingual model; Tamil must be benchmarked | Streaming | **US$0.017/minute** |
| **Google Cloud Speech-to-Text Standard** | Yes | Multilingual cloud STT | Real-time/standard | **US$0.016/minute** |
| **Google Dynamic Batch** | Yes | Low-priority batch processing | Batch | **US$0.003/minute** |
| **Amazon Transcribe streaming** | Yes | General cloud STT | Streaming | **US$0.01/minute** |
| **Amazon Transcribe batch** | Yes | Recorded-file transcription | Batch | **US$0.006/minute** |
| **Claude API** | No standalone STT API found | Claude can analyse text after transcription | Not applicable | No audio-minute STT price |
| **Perplexity API** | No standalone STT API found | Search, Sonar, Agent and Embeddings APIs | Not applicable | No audio-minute STT price |

Sarvam currently charges by the second, rounded to the nearest second. It provides ₹100 of free credits for new accounts. Its recommended Saaras v3 model supports transcribe, translate, verbatim, transliteration and code-mixed output. citeturn994629view1turn994629view2

Google currently publishes US$0.016 per minute for standard recognition and US$0.003 per minute for dynamic batch recognition. citeturn772691search0 Amazon Transcribe publishes US$0.01 per minute for streaming and US$0.006 per minute for batch processing in its cited example. citeturn772691search2 OpenAI currently publishes US$0.017 per minute for its streaming speech-to-text model. citeturn890252search0

Anthropic’s published API overview lists Messages, Batch, Token Counting and Models APIs, but does not list a dedicated speech-to-text endpoint. citeturn772691search7 Perplexity’s official API currently focuses on Agent, Sonar, Search and Embeddings rather than a native speech-recognition service. citeturn586086search7turn586086search26

---

# 3. Sarvam’s effective per-minute cost

| Usage | Basic transcription | With speaker identification |
|---|---:|---:|
| 1 minute | ₹0.50 | ₹0.75 |
| 10 minutes | ₹5 | ₹7.50 |
| 1 hour | ₹30 | ₹45 |
| 10 hours | ₹300 | ₹450 |
| 100 hours | ₹3,000 | ₹4,500 |
| 1,000 hours | ₹30,000 | ₹45,000 |

For a two-person telephone call, speaker identification can be valuable because the output can distinguish:

- Agent
- Customer

That makes it easier to create:

- Customer summary
- Agent action
- Complaint type
- Follow-up task
- QA assessment

---

# 4. Why Sarvam is especially relevant

Sarvam is not simply saying “we support Tamil.”

Its speech system is specifically positioned for:

- 22 Indian languages
- Automatic language detection
- Code-mixing
- Multiple speakers
- Telephony optimisation
- 8 kHz telephone audio
- Native-script transcription
- Romanised output
- English translation
- Batch recordings
- Real-time streaming

The API accepts common formats including MP3, WAV, AAC, OGG, FLAC, M4A, AMR, WMA and WebM. Batch files can be up to two hours long, while streaming supports 8 kHz or 16 kHz WAV/raw PCM audio. citeturn994629view2

That is directly relevant to your use case because your recordings may contain:

- Tamil
- English
- Tamil-English code-switching
- Regional pronunciation
- Product names
- Phone numbers
- Addresses
- Multiple speakers
- Telephone-quality audio

---

# 5. Can Sarvam be tested without building a full application?

Yes.

## Stage 1 — Browser test

1. Create a Sarvam account.
2. Use the free credits.
3. Open its speech-to-text testing interface.
4. Upload or record a short Tamil sample.
5. Try these output modes:
   - Tamil transcription
   - Code-mixed output
   - Transliteration
   - English translation
6. Compare the results manually.

New accounts currently receive ₹100 in credits. At ₹30 per hour, that theoretically covers more than three hours of ordinary STT, subject to actual billing and other API usage. citeturn994629view1

## Stage 2 — Controlled benchmark

Do not test one clean recording and decide.

Prepare at least 20 samples:

| Test group | Samples |
|---|---:|
| Clear Tamil speech | 3 |
| Tamil-English mixed speech | 4 |
| Tirunelveli/regional accent | 3 |
| Telephone recording | 3 |
| Two-speaker conversation | 3 |
| Background noise | 2 |
| Names, addresses and numbers | 2 |

Run the same files through:

- Sarvam
- OpenAI
- Google Cloud Speech-to-Text
- Amazon Transcribe, where Tamil support/configuration is suitable

Then score:

| Measure | Weight |
|---|---:|
| Tamil-word accuracy | 25% |
| English code-mix accuracy | 15% |
| Names and numbers | 15% |
| Speaker separation | 10% |
| Punctuation/readability | 10% |
| Telephone-audio quality | 10% |
| Processing speed | 5% |
| Cost | 5% |
| Integration simplicity | 5% |

Sarvam should not be selected merely because it is Indian. It should win your own real-audio test.

---

# 6. Recommended production transcription flow

For your BPO model:

```text
Telephone call
      ↓
Exotel / Tata Smartflo / Amazon Connect records audio
      ↓
Audio sent to Sarvam batch or streaming API
      ↓
Tamil/Tamil-English transcript
      ↓
Speaker separation
      ↓
Rules extract client, customer and urgency
      ↓
AI produces summary and proposed priority
      ↓
Human checks critical cases
      ↓
Zoho Desk/CRM ticket created or updated
```

Use the speech engine for transcription and a separate reasoning model for:

- Summarisation
- Intent classification
- Priority recommendation
- Action extraction
- Knowledge-base suggestions

You could use Sarvam for transcription and ChatGPT/Claude for higher-level reasoning. You do not have to choose only one AI company.

---

# 7. Recommended transcription strategy

## For live telephone calls

Use:

- Sarvam streaming STT or a contact-centre’s integrated transcription
- Rule-based keyword detection
- Human override for high-priority matters

## For uploaded recordings

Use:

- Sarvam Batch API
- Diarisation for multiple speakers
- Store transcript against the customer ticket

## For long meeting recordings

Use:

- Batch transcription
- Speaker identification
- Then send the transcript to an LLM for:
  - Decisions
  - Tasks
  - Risks
  - Responsibilities
  - Follow-up dates

## For critical fields

Always verify:

- Phone number
- Amount
- Order number
- Address
- Patient/customer name
- Date
- Bank or transaction reference

No speech engine should be trusted blindly for these.

---

# Part B — Amazon Connect and Microsoft diallers

## 8. Correction: Amazon Connect does have a dialler

Amazon Connect is not just an IVR or inbound-call system.

It currently supports **Outbound Campaigns**, including:

- Preview dialling
- Priority-ordered contacts
- Campaign segmentation
- Reattempt logic
- Agentless outbound calling
- Voice campaigns
- Multi-step journeys
- SMS
- Email
- WhatsApp
- Campaign dashboards
- Contact-history integration

Amazon added preview dialling and has long supported agentless outbound campaigns. Its release notes also show priority ordering and multi-channel journey capabilities. citeturn994629view3

Therefore, any earlier statement suggesting that Amazon Connect “does not have a dialler” was incorrect or insufficiently explained.

### The accurate answer

> **Amazon Connect has inbound calling and outbound dialling/campaign capabilities.**

However, its availability and supported destinations vary by AWS region. AWS documents regional restrictions for predictive/progressive outbound calling. citeturn890252search2

For India, the key question is not merely whether Amazon has a dialler. The questions are:

- Can it provide the required Indian telephone numbers?
- Can India-to-India calling be provisioned directly?
- Which carrier/partner is required?
- Are outbound campaign modes supported for the selected region and destination?
- Does the architecture comply with Indian telecom requirements?

That is why Exotel, Tata Smartflo, Airtel IQ or another Indian telecom provider may still be required as the telephony layer.

---

# 9. Amazon Connect is a complete contact-centre platform

It can provide:

| Function | Amazon Connect |
|---|---:|
| Inbound calls | Yes |
| IVR and routing flows | Yes |
| Agent desktop | Yes |
| Queues | Yes |
| Callback | Yes |
| Outbound campaigns | Yes |
| Preview dialling | Yes |
| Agentless outbound | Yes |
| Cases | Yes |
| Tasks | Yes |
| Customer profiles | Yes |
| Recording/transcription | Yes |
| Analytics | Yes |
| Workforce management | Yes/additional capability |
| AI agent assistance | Yes |
| WhatsApp/email/SMS journeys | Yes, where supported |

Amazon Connect is technically closer to what you are designing than a simple phone dialler. It has queues, customer profiles, tasks, cases, outbound campaigns and AI capabilities. Its latest documentation includes customer-context passing, cases and post-contact guided workflows. citeturn994629view3

The issue is that configuring it requires more cloud and integration expertise than buying a basic Indian dialler.

---

# 10. Microsoft also has diallers

Microsoft Dynamics 365 Contact Center includes an outbound dialler. Agents can search contacts, select an outbound profile and make calls from the service workspace. Microsoft also supports inbound/outbound profiles, caller ID, call recording and transcription. citeturn890252search3turn890252search20

Dynamics 365 Sales also introduced an embedded dialler built on Azure Communication Services, supporting outbound and inbound calls from CRM records. citeturn890252search7

So the correct comparison is:

| Vendor | Contact-centre/dialler product |
|---|---|
| Amazon | Amazon Connect |
| Microsoft | Dynamics 365 Contact Center + Azure Communication Services |
| Google | Google Cloud Contact Center AI integrations; Google Voice is separate and not offered in India |
| Zoho | Zoho Voice/Telephony integrations, CRM and Desk |
| Indian providers | Exotel, Tata Smartflo, Airtel IQ, Knowlarity, Ozonetel, Ameyo |

---

# 11. Why Amazon and Microsoft sometimes appear not to have “a dialler”

Because they do not always market it as a simple standalone product such as:

> “Buy ten dialler licences.”

They market a larger platform:

- Contact centre
- Voice channel
- Outbound campaigns
- Customer engagement
- Communications APIs
- Omnichannel service

Inside that platform is the dialler.

Indian vendors often make the term **dialler** more prominent because their buyers directly ask for:

- Predictive dialler
- Auto dialler
- OBD
- IVR
- Virtual number
- Call recording

---

# 12. What should you test first?

Do not test ten systems simultaneously.

## Test 1 — Transcription

Take 20 real Tamil/Tamil-English recordings and test:

1. Sarvam
2. OpenAI
3. Google STT

Evaluate actual accuracy and cost.

## Test 2 — Five-agent Indian telephony

Obtain demonstrations from:

1. Exotel
2. Tata Smartflo
3. Ozonetel or Knowlarity

Test:

- Inbound IVR
- Callback task
- Recording
- Call log
- API/webhook
- Caller identity
- Connected-call rate
- CRM integration

## Test 3 — Orchestration prototype

Connect:

- Telephony
- Sarvam transcription
- Zoho Desk
- Zoho Creator/CRM

Workflow:

```text
Inbound call
→ record request
→ transcribe
→ classify
→ create ticket
→ assign priority
→ available worker calls back
→ notes updated
→ ticket closed
```

That single working pilot will answer more questions than another broad comparison.

---

# 13. My recommendation at this point

## Speech-to-text

**Start by testing Sarvam.**

Reasons:

- ₹0.50 per minute
- Indian-language specialisation
- Tamil and code-mixing
- Telephony optimisation
- Batch and streaming
- Diarisation
- English translation
- Low-cost free-credit evaluation

Do not commit exclusively until it is benchmarked against your actual Tirunelveli/Tamil-English recordings.

## India telephony

Begin with:

- Exotel or Tata Smartflo for the Indian voice layer

## Customer history

Use:

- Zoho Desk and Zoho CRM

## Unique orchestration

Use:

- Zoho Creator initially

## Advanced AI

Use:

- Sarvam for speech
- Rules for known classifications
- ChatGPT/Claude for uncertain summaries and intent
- Human approval for critical classifications

---

# 14. The simplified architecture

```text
Exotel / Tata Smartflo
        │
        ├── Inbound and outbound calls
        ├── IVR
        ├── Recording
        └── Callback
                ↓
           Sarvam AI
        ├── Tamil transcription
        ├── Code-mix handling
        ├── Speaker separation
        └── English translation
                ↓
       Rule + AI decision layer
        ├── Intent
        ├── Priority
        ├── Summary
        └── Required skill
                ↓
       Zoho Creator / Desk / CRM
        ├── Work item
        ├── Customer history
        ├── Allocation
        ├── SLA
        └── Handover
                ↓
      Flexible women workforce
```

You do not need to solve Google, Amazon, Microsoft, Sarvam, OpenAI, Claude, Perplexity and the entire BPO architecture in one purchase.

The practical starting stack is:

> **Indian telephony + Sarvam transcription + Zoho workflow + AI-assisted classification + human review.**

That is the smallest architecture that directly tests your actual business idea.
